> /iterate
> Change default --vertex-tolerance to 5e-5.
> When visualizing cones in viz mode, make sure they&#x27;re at least 2x the maximum dimension of the model in length.
> Make stage 2 debugging printing more consistent:
> - With verbose=1: just summaries such as &quot;Stage 2.1 (0.001s): Deduced 60 planar hypotheses (24 multi-face, 36 single-face) covering 156 of 156 mesh faces&quot;
> - verbose=2: include hypothesis such as &quot;  Plane 10: 2 faces, 4 vertices, normal=[0.8804, -0.0577, 0.4708], d=194.8917, err_max=4.66e-6, err_min=-4.66e-6&quot;
> - verbose=3: for each successful hypothesis, print seed faces and final number of faces and details of acceptance/rejection.
> - verbose=4: for each seed attempted, print seed faces and why the seed is rejected or BFS searched. For accepted seeds, print the fit and error.
> - verbose=5: for each seed BFS searched, for each face explored, print the face&#x27;s error, if there&#x27;s a refit say so and print the refit results/error, and whether the face is accepted or rejected.

> --viz-stages-level=1: Stop at the end of each accepted hypothesis.
> --viz-stages-level=2: Also stop at the beginning of each accepted seed.
> --viz-stages-level=3: Also stop at each explored BFS face that isn&#x27;t skipped.
> --viz-stages-level=4: Also stop at the beginning of every seed candidate (accepted or not) and at each explored BFS face, even if it&#x27;s skipped.

## viz.rs — Structures and Event Handling

### Key Enums/Structs

**`VizAction`** — [viz.rs L700-L710](src/viz.rs#L700-L710):
```rust
pub enum VizAction {
    NextStep,       // space bar
    NextSeed,       // shift+space
    SkipToSeed(usize),  // click on a face → skip to seed containing that face
    Quit,           // Q or Esc
}
```

**`VizOverlay`** — [viz.rs L791-L808](src/viz.rs#L791-L808):
```rust
pub struct VizOverlay {
    pub face_highlights: Vec<FaceHighlight>,
    pub edge_highlights: Vec<EdgeHighlight>,
    pub cylinders: Vec<CylinderOverlay>,
    pub cones: Vec<ConeOverlay>,
    pub spheres: Vec<SphereOverlay>,
    pub tori: Vec<TorusOverlay>,
    pub lines: Vec<LineOverlay>,
    pub shape_meshes: Vec<ShapeMeshOverlay>,
    pub status_text: String,
    pub focus_point: Option<[f32; 3]>,
    pub focus_normal: Option<[f32; 3]>,
}
```

**Overlay types** — [viz.rs L711-L790](src/viz.rs#L711-L790):
- `FaceHighlight` — face indices + RGBA color
- `EdgeHighlight` — face indices + RGBA color (thick edge outlines)
- `CylinderOverlay` — origin, direction, radius, half_length, color
- `ConeOverlay` — apex, direction, half_angle, t_min, t_max, color
- `SphereOverlay` — center, radius, color
- `TorusOverlay` — center, direction, major_radius, minor_radius, color
- `LineOverlay` — positions (pairs), color, no_depth_test
- `ShapeMeshOverlay` — positions, normals, indices, color, edge_positions, edge_color

**`VizSender`** — [viz.rs L846-L850](src/viz.rs#L846-L850):
```rust
pub struct VizSender {
    overlay_tx: mpsc::SyncSender<VizOverlay>,
    action_rx: mpsc::Receiver<VizAction>,
}
```
Key method `show_and_wait()` — [viz.rs L854-L862](src/viz.rs#L854-L862): prints `[viz] <status_text>` to stderr, sends overlay, blocks until user responds.

### How `viz_active` is checked

In `mod.rs`, each stage checks `config.viz_active(stage, substage)`:
```rust
let viz_21 = if config.viz_active(2, 1) { viz } else { None };
let viz_22 = if config.viz_active(2, 2) { viz } else { None };
let viz_23 = if config.viz_active(2, 3) { viz } else { None };
let viz_25 = if config.viz_active(2, 5) { viz } else { None };
let viz_26 = if config.viz_active(2, 6) { viz } else { None };
```
If `viz_active` is false, `None` is passed, and all `if let Some(viz_sender) = viz` blocks in bfs.rs are skipped.

### Click-to-skip logic (`SkipToSeed`)

**Render loop side** — [viz.rs L1620-L1637](src/viz.rs#L1620-L1637):
- On `MouseRelease` (left button), if the drag distance is < 5 pixels (click not drag), and `waiting_for_input` is true:
  - Ray-casts via `pick_face()` to find the face under the cursor
  - Sends `VizAction::SkipToSeed(face_index)` on the action channel
  - Sets `waiting_for_input = false`

**BFS side** — [bfs.rs L340-L345](src/stage2/bfs.rs#L340-L345):
- `let mut skip_to_face: Option<usize> = None;`
- When viz receives `SkipToSeed(face)`: sets `skip_to_face = Some(face)` and `skip_viz = true`
- Each subsequent seed is auto-skipped unless `seed.faces.contains(&target)`, at which point `skip_to_face = None` and visualization resumes

### Render loop event handling

[viz.rs L1555-L1674](src/viz.rs#L1555-L1674) — key events:
- **Space**: sends `NextStep` (or `NextSeed` with shift)
- **Q/Escape**: exits
- **P**: toggle perspective/orthographic
- **E**: toggle edges (Shift+E for soft edges)
- **S**: toggle solid
- **H**: toggle edge hiding
- **C**: toggle auto-center
- **N**: toggle face numbers
- **?**: toggle help overlay

Mouse events:
- Left drag: orbit (rotation around target)
- Right drag: pan
- Scroll: zoom toward cursor
- Left click (no drag): `SkipToSeed`

### Overlay receive & GPU build

[viz.rs L1434-L1530](src/viz.rs#L1434-L1530) — on `try_recv()` success:
- Builds GPU objects for each overlay type (face highlights, edge highlights, cylinders, cones, spheres, tori, lines, shape meshes)
- Sets `waiting_for_input = true`
- Starts camera animation if `focus_normal` is set and auto-center is on

### Camera animation

[viz.rs L1399-L1430](src/viz.rs#L1399-L1430) — rotation-only slerp, 300ms duration, only triggers if angle > 45°. Clamped to 15° from Z-poles.

### Rendering

## How bfs.rs sends viz overlays

Two main viz sends in `generate_hypotheses()`:

1. **Seed visualization** — [bfs.rs L346-L396](src/stage2/bfs.rs#L346-L396): Shows seed faces (orange/gold), surface overlay, gray background for committed faces. Status: `"BFS-{type}: seed=(...) {fit_desc}"`.

2. **Accepted face visualization** — [bfs.rs L542-L602](src/stage2/bfs.rs#L542-L602): Shows seed (orange), previously-accepted (blue), just-accepted (dark blue), committed (gray), surface overlay. Status: `"BFS-{type}: accepted fi={n} ({count} faces) {fit_desc}"`.

Both use `show_and_wait()` and handle all 4 `VizAction` variants: `Quit` → return early, `NextSeed` → skip remaining BFS viz for this seed, `SkipToSeed(face)` → set `skip_to_face`, `NextStep` → continue normally.

```
**Render loop side** — [viz.rs L1620-L1637](src/viz.rs#L1620-L1637):
- On `MouseRelease` (left button), if the drag distance is < 5 pixels (click not drag), and `waiting_for_input` is true:
  - Ray-casts via `pick_face()` to find the face under the cursor
  - Sends `VizAction::SkipToSeed(face_index)` on the action channel
  - Sets `waiting_for_input = false`

**BFS side** — [bfs.rs L340-L345](src/stage2/bfs.rs#L340-L345):
- `let mut skip_to_face: Option<usize> = None;`
- When viz receives `SkipToSeed(face)`: sets `skip_to_face = Some(face)` and `skip_viz = true`
- Each subsequent seed is auto-skipped unless `seed.faces.contains(&target)`, at which point `skip_to_face = None` and visualization resumes

### Render loop event handling

[viz.rs L1555-L1674](src/viz.rs#L1555-L1674) — key events:
- **Space**: sends `NextStep` (or `NextSeed` with shift)
- **Q/Escape**: exits
- **P**: toggle perspective/orthographic
- **E**: toggle edges (Shift+E for soft edges)
- **S**: toggle solid
- **H**: toggle edge hiding
- **C**: toggle auto-center
- **N**: toggle face numbers
- **?**: toggle help overlay

Mouse events:
- Left drag: orbit (rotation around target)
- Right drag: pan
- Scroll: zoom toward cursor
- Left click (no drag): `SkipToSeed`

### Overlay receive & GPU build

[viz.rs L1434-L1530](src/viz.rs#L1434-L1530) — on `try_recv()` success:
- Builds GPU objects for each overlay type (face highlights, edge highlights, cylinders, cones, spheres, tori, lines, shape meshes)
- Sets `waiting_for_input = true`
- Starts camera animation if `focus_normal` is set and auto-center is on
```

```
//! Generic BFS hypothesis generation framework.
//!
//! Provides a trait-based abstraction for surface hypothesis BFS, parameterized
//! by surface type. Each surface type (planar, conical, spherical, toroidal)
//! implements the `SurfaceType` trait, and the shared `generate_hypotheses()`
//! function handles the BFS expansion loop.
//!
//! Ported from the C++ `generate_hypotheses<Traits>()` template in brepper-cpp2.

use std::collections::{HashSet, VecDeque};

use crate::stage1::{ConnectedMesh, MeshFace, NO_HYPOTHESIS};
use crate::viz::{self, VizAction, VizSender};

use super::{dot3, face_area, face_centroid, viz_face_centroid, viz_face_normal, REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// BFS configuration
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Configuration for the BFS hypothesis generation.
pub(super) struct BfsConfig {
    pub vertex_tol: f64,
    pub surface_tol: f64,
    pub angular_tol: f64,
    pub verbosity: u8,
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Surface type trait
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A seed for BFS expansion: a set of face indices to start from.
pub(super) struct Seed {
    pub faces: Vec<usize>,
}

/// Trait that each surface type implements to parameterize the BFS.
///
/// The generic `generate_hypotheses()` function calls these methods to:
/// - Generate seed face sets for each starting face
/// - Fit a surface to a set of faces/vertices
/// - Compute vertex and centroid distances to the fitted surface
/// - Check convexity consistency
/// - Validate angular coverage and other acceptance criteria
/// - Construct the final hypothesis struct
pub(super) trait SurfaceType {
    /// The fitted surface parameters (e.g., axis, radius, center).
    type Fit: Clone;
    /// The output hypothesis struct.
    type Hypothesis;

    /// Human-readable name for logging (e.g., "planar", "conical", "spherical").
    fn name() -> &'static str;

    /// Access the hypothesis index field on a MeshFace (read).
    fn hypothesis_field(face: &MeshFace) -> i32;

    /// Access the hypothesis index field on a MeshFace (write).
    fn set_hypothesis_field(face: &mut MeshFace, value: i32);

    /// The "undeduced" sentinel value for this surface type's hypothesis field.
    fn undeduced_value() -> i32;

    /// Check whether a face is eligible for BFS expansion (not already claimed
    /// by a prior hypothesis or excluded by other surface types).
    fn face_eligible(face: &MeshFace) -> bool {
        Self::hypothesis_field(face) == Self::undeduced_value()
    }

    /// Generate seed face sets anchored at face `fi`.
    /// Returns an empty vec if `fi` is not a valid seed anchor.
    fn generate_seeds(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize, config: &BfsConfig) -> Vec<Seed>;

    /// Fit a surface to the given faces and vertices.
    /// Returns None if fitting fails.
    fn fit_surface(
        mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
        face_list: &[usize],
        vertex_list: &[usize],
        verbosity: u8,
    ) -> Option<Self::Fit>;

    /// Compute the absolute distance from a vertex position to the fitted surface.
    fn vertex_distance(fit: &Self::Fit, pos: &[f64; 3]) -> f64;

    /// Compute the absolute distance from a centroid to the fitted surface.
    /// Default: same as vertex_distance.
    fn centroid_distance(fit: &Self::Fit, centroid: &[f64; 3]) -> f64 {
        Self::vertex_distance(fit, centroid)
    }

    /// Check convexity consistency: does `fi` match the convexity of the fit?
    /// Return true to accept, false to reject.
    /// Default implementation always accepts (e.g., for planar surfaces).
    fn convexity_check(_fit: &Self::Fit, _mesh: &ConnectedMesh, _fi: usize) -> bool {
        true
    }

    /// Determine convexity from a set of faces and update the fit.
```

```
pub fn stage2(config: &Config, mut mesh: ConnectedMesh, viz: Option<&crate::viz::VizSender>) -> Result<Stage2Output, Stage2Error> {
    // Stage 2.1: Deduce planar hypotheses
    let t = Instant::now();
    let viz_21 = if config.viz_active(2, 1) { viz } else { None };
    let bfs_config = BfsConfig {
        vertex_tol: config.vertex_tolerance_mm,
        surface_tol: config.surface_tolerance_mm,
        angular_tol: config.angular_tolerance_rad,
        verbosity: config.verbosity,
    };
    let (mut planar_hypotheses, planar_quit) = bfs::generate_hypotheses::<PlanarSurface>(&mut mesh, &bfs_config, viz_21);

    // Synthesize single-face planar hypotheses for faces not covered by BFS.
    // BFS with acceptance >= 2 only produces multi-face hypotheses; every
    // remaining face still needs a planar fallback for stage 2.6 selection.
    for fi in 0..mesh.faces.len() {
        if mesh.faces[fi].planar_hypothesis < 0 {
            let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
            let n = face.normal.unwrap();
            let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
            let verts: Vec<usize> = face.vertex_indices[..vc].to_vec();
            let sum_d: f64 = verts.iter()
                .map(|&vi| {
                    let v = &mesh.vertices[vi];
                    n[0] * v.x + n[1] * v.y + n[2] * v.z
                })
                .sum();
            let distance = sum_d / verts.len() as f64;
            let new_idx = planar_hypotheses.len() as i32;
            planar_hypotheses.push(PlanarHypothesis {
                normal: n,
                distance,
                faces: vec![fi],
                vertices: verts,
                error_max: 0.0,
                error_min: 0.0,
                error_abs_sum: 0.0,
            });
            mesh.faces[fi].planar_hypothesis = new_idx;
        }
    }

    if !config.quiet {
        let multi_face_count = planar_hypotheses.iter().filter(|h| h.faces.len() > 1).count();
        let single_face_count = planar_hypotheses.len() - multi_face_count;
        let covered_faces: usize = planar_hypotheses.iter().map(|h| h.faces.len()).sum();
        eprintln!(
            "Stage 2.1 ({:.3}s): Deduced {} planar hypotheses ({} multi-face, {} single-face) covering {} of {} mesh faces",
            t.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),
            planar_hypotheses.len(),
            multi_face_count,
            single_face_count,
            covered_faces,
            mesh.faces.len(),
        );
        if config.verbose {
            for (i, h) in planar_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
                if h.faces.len() > 1 {
                    eprintln!(
                        "  Plane {}: {} faces, {} vertices, normal=[{:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}], d={:.4}, err_max={:.2e}, err_min={:.2e}",
                        i, h.faces.len(), h.vertices.len(),
```

```
                    );
                }
            }
        }
    }

        if !config.quiet {
            eprintln!("  Compare: all conical/cylindrical hypothesis centroids within tolerance");
        }
    }

        return Ok(Stage2Output {
            mesh,
            planar_hypotheses,
            conical_hypotheses,
```

Now let me fix the cone visualization length. The  currently computes t_min/t_max from the face centroids and adds 20% padding. We need it to be at least 2x the maximum dimension of the model. I need to pass the bounding box info to the cone overlay function:

<details>
<summary>Reading lines 570-600 of <a href="../src/stage2/mod.rs#L570-L601">mod.rs</a></summary>

```

/// Create a ConeOverlay spanning the axial extent of the given faces (with padding).
pub(crate) fn centered_cone_overlay(
    apex: [f64; 3], direction: [f64; 3], half_angle: f64,
    face_list: &[usize], mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    color: [f32; 4],
) -> viz::ConeOverlay {
    // Compute axial extent of face centroids relative to apex
    let mut t_min = f64::INFINITY;
    let mut t_max = f64::NEG_INFINITY;
    for &fi in face_list {
        let c = face_centroid(&mesh.faces[fi], &mesh.vertices);
        let d = [c[0] - apex[0], c[1] - apex[1], c[2] - apex[2]];
        let t = dot3(&d, &direction);
        t_min = t_min.min(t);
        t_max = t_max.max(t);
    }
    // Pad by 20% of the axial span for visual clarity
    let span = t_max - t_min;
    let pad = span * 0.2;
    viz::ConeOverlay {
        apex,
        direction,
        half_angle,
        t_min: t_min - pad,
        t_max: t_max + pad,
        color,
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
```
</details>

I need to ensure the cone extends at least 2x the maximum dimension. The simplest approach is to compute a minimum half-length from the bounding box. Let me update the  to use the mesh bounding box:

<details>
<summary>Edited: <a href="../src/stage2/mod.rs#L571-L604">mod.rs -28+33</a></summary>

```
{
  "edits": [
    {
      "content": "/// Create a ConeOverlay spanning the axial extent of the given faces (with padding).\n/// Ensures the cone is at least 2x the maximum model dimension in length.\npub(crate) fn centered_cone_overlay(\n    apex: [f64; 3], direction: [f64; 3], half_angle: f64,\n    face_list: &[usize], mesh: &ConnectedMesh,\n    color: [f32; 4],\n) -> viz::ConeOverlay {\n    // Compute axial extent of face centroids relative to apex\n    let mut t_min = f64::INFINITY;\n    let mut t_max = f64::NEG_INFINITY;\n    for &fi in face_list {\n        let c = face_centroid(&mesh.faces[fi], &mesh.vertices);\n        let d = [c[0] - apex[0], c[1] - apex[1], c[2] - apex[2]];\n        let t = dot3(&d, &direction);\n        t_min = t_min.min(t);\n        t_max = t_max.max(t);\n    }\n    // Ensure the cone is at least 2x the maximum model dimension in length\n    let bb_diag = bounding_box_diagonal(&mesh.vertices);\n    let min_half_length = bb_diag; // 2x max dimension means full length = 2 * bb_diag, so half_length = bb_diag\n    let center_t = (t_min + t_max) * 0.5;\n    let half_span = ((t_max - t_min) * 0.5).max(min_half_length);\n    // Pad by 20% for visual clarity\n    let pad = half_span * 0.2;\n    viz::ConeOverlay {\n        apex,\n        direction,\n        half_angle,\n        t_min: center_t - half_span - pad,\n        t_max: center_t + half_span + pad,\n        color,\n    }\n}",
```

```
//! Generic BFS hypothesis generation framework.
//!
//! Provides a trait-based abstraction for surface hypothesis BFS, parameterized
//! by surface type. Each surface type (planar, conical, spherical, toroidal)
//! implements the `SurfaceType` trait, and the shared `generate_hypotheses()`
//! function handles the BFS expansion loop.
//!
//! Ported from the C++ `generate_hypotheses<Traits>()` template in brepper-cpp2.

use std::collections::{HashSet, VecDeque};

use crate::stage1::{ConnectedMesh, MeshFace, NO_HYPOTHESIS};
use crate::viz::{self, VizAction, VizSender};

use super::{dot3, face_area, face_centroid, viz_face_centroid, viz_face_normal, REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// BFS configuration
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Configuration for the BFS hypothesis generation.
pub(super) struct BfsConfig {
    pub vertex_tol: f64,
    pub surface_tol: f64,
    pub angular_tol: f64,
    pub verbosity: u8,
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Surface type trait
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A seed for BFS expansion: a set of face indices to start from.
pub(super) struct Seed {
    pub faces: Vec<usize>,
}

/// Trait that each surface type implements to parameterize the BFS.
///
/// The generic `generate_hypotheses()` function calls these methods to:
/// - Generate seed face sets for each starting face
/// - Fit a surface to a set of faces/vertices
/// - Compute vertex and centroid distances to the fitted surface
/// - Check convexity consistency
/// - Validate angular coverage and other acceptance criteria
/// - Construct the final hypothesis struct
pub(super) trait SurfaceType {
    /// The fitted surface parameters (e.g., axis, radius, center).
    type Fit: Clone;
    /// The output hypothesis struct.
    type Hypothesis;

    /// Human-readable name for logging (e.g., "planar", "conical", "spherical").
    fn name() -> &'static str;

    /// Access the hypothesis index field on a MeshFace (read).
    fn hypothesis_field(face: &MeshFace) -> i32;

    /// Access the hypothesis index field on a MeshFace (write).
    fn set_hypothesis_field(face: &mut MeshFace, value: i32);

    /// The "undeduced" sentinel value for this surface type's hypothesis field.
    fn undeduced_value() -> i32;

    /// Check whether a face is eligible for BFS expansion (not already claimed
    /// by a prior hypothesis or excluded by other surface types).
    fn face_eligible(face: &MeshFace) -> bool {
        Self::hypothesis_field(face) == Self::undeduced_value()
    }

    /// Generate seed face sets anchored at face `fi`.
    /// Returns an empty vec if `fi` is not a valid seed anchor.
    fn generate_seeds(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize, config: &BfsConfig) -> Vec<Seed>;

    /// Fit a surface to the given faces and vertices.
    /// Returns None if fitting fails.
    fn fit_surface(
        mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
        face_list: &[usize],
        vertex_list: &[usize],
        verbosity: u8,
    ) -> Option<Self::Fit>;

    /// Compute the absolute distance from a vertex position to the fitted surface.
    fn vertex_distance(fit: &Self::Fit, pos: &[f64; 3]) -> f64;

    /// Compute the absolute distance from a centroid to the fitted surface.
    /// Default: same as vertex_distance.
    fn centroid_distance(fit: &Self::Fit, centroid: &[f64; 3]) -> f64 {
        Self::vertex_distance(fit, centroid)
    }

    /// Check convexity consistency: does `fi` match the convexity of the fit?
    /// Return true to accept, false to reject.
    /// Default implementation always accepts (e.g., for planar surfaces).
    fn convexity_check(_fit: &Self::Fit, _mesh: &ConnectedMesh, _fi: usize) -> bool {
        true
    }

    /// Determine convexity from a set of faces and update the fit.
```
</details>

<details>
<summary>Reading lines 100-250 of <a href="../src/stage2/bfs.rs#L100-L251">bfs.rs</a></summary>

```
    /// Determine convexity from a set of faces and update the fit.
    /// Default: no-op (for surface types without convexity).
    fn determine_convexity(_mesh: &ConnectedMesh, _face_list: &[usize], _fit: &mut Self::Fit) {}

    /// Post-BFS acceptance test (e.g., angular coverage, minimum face count).
    /// Return true to accept the hypothesis.
    fn acceptance_test(
        mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
        face_list: &[usize],
        fit: &Self::Fit,
        config: &BfsConfig,
    ) -> bool;

    /// Multiplier for seed vertex tolerance.  Defaults to 1.0.
    /// Cone seeds are inherently noisier (mixed-face apex neighborhoods)
    /// so they override this to allow a looser seed threshold.
    fn seed_vtol_multiplier() -> f64 { 1.0 }

    /// Multiplier for the angular tolerance used in dihedral-angle checks.
    /// Defaults to 1.0.  Cones override to 2.0 because tessellated cone tips
    /// have inter-strip dihedral angles that exceed the default tolerance.
    fn angular_tol_multiplier() -> f64 { 1.0 }

    /// Generate visualization overlays (cylinder/cone/sphere/torus) from the current fit.
    /// Default: no overlays (e.g., for planar surfaces).
    fn viz_overlays(
        _fit: &Self::Fit,
        _mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
        _face_list: &[usize],
    ) -> (Vec<viz::CylinderOverlay>, Vec<viz::ConeOverlay>, Vec<viz::SphereOverlay>, Vec<viz::TorusOverlay>) {
        (Vec::new(), Vec::new(), Vec::new(), Vec::new())
    }

    /// Short description of the current fit for viz status text.
    /// Default: just the surface type name.
    fn viz_fit_description(_fit: &Self::Fit) -> String {
        Self::name().to_string()
    }

    /// Construct the final hypothesis from the BFS result.
    fn make_hypothesis(
        mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
        faces: Vec<usize>,
        vertices: Vec<usize>,
        fit: &Self::Fit,
    ) -> Self::Hypothesis;
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Dihedral angle computation
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Compute the dihedral angle in radians between two adjacent faces.
fn dihedral_angle(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize, fj: usize) -> f64 {
    let n1 = match mesh.faces[fi].normal {
        Some(n) => n,
        None => return 0.0,
    };
    let n2 = match mesh.faces[fj].normal {
        Some(n) => n,
        None => return 0.0,
    };
    let d = dot3(&n1, &n2).clamp(-1.0, 1.0);
    d.acos()
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Generic BFS hypothesis generation
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Run BFS hypothesis generation for a given surface type.

/// Iterates through all faces, generating seeds for each eligible face,

) -> (Vec<S::Hypothesis>, bool)

    let mut acceptance_failures = 0_u32;

    let mut prof_total_seed_count = 0_u64;
```

```
/// Check if two faces are neighbors (share an edge).
pub(super) fn is_neighbor(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize, fj: usize) -> bool {
    let f = &mesh.faces[fi];
    let vc = f.vertex_count as usize;
    for &n in &f.neighbors[..vc] {
        if n >= 0 && n as usize == fj {
            return true;
        }
```

```
            "  [{}-BFS] stats: {} seeds generated, {} fit failures, {} tried",
            S::name(), seeds_generated, fit_failures, seeds_tried
        );
        eprintln!(
            "    stol fail: {}, angle fail: {}, accept fail: {}, committed: {}",
            seed_stol_failures, seed_angle_failures, acceptance_failures, committed
        );
    }

    // PROFILING: final summary
    let prof_total = prof_start.elapsed();
    eprintln!(
        "  [{}-BFS PROFILE SUMMARY] total={:.3}s, faces_visited={}, total_seeds={}, max_seeds_per_face={}",
        S::name(), prof_total.as_secs_f64(), prof_faces_visited, prof_total_seed_count, prof_max_seeds_per_face,
    );
    eprintln!(
        "    seed_gen={:.3}s, seed_fit={:.3}s ({} calls), bfs={:.3}s (total_bfs_faces={}, max={})",
        prof_time_seed_gen.as_secs_f64(), prof_time_seed_fit.as_secs_f64(), prof_total_fit_calls,
        prof_time_bfs.as_secs_f64(), prof_total_bfs_faces, prof_max_bfs_faces,
    );
    eprintln!(
        "    refit={:.3}s ({} calls), final_fit={:.3}s, acceptance={:.3}s",
        prof_time_refit.as_secs_f64(), prof_total_refit_calls,
        prof_time_final_fit.as_secs_f64(), prof_time_acceptance.as_secs_f64(),
    );
    eprintln!(
        "    avg_seed_fit={:.3}ms, avg_refit={:.3}ms",
        if prof_total_fit_calls > 0 { prof_time_seed_fit.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0 / prof_total_fit_calls as f64 } else { 0.0 },
        if prof_total_refit_calls > 0 { prof_time_refit.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0 / prof_total_refit_calls as f64 } else { 0.0 },
    );

    (hypotheses, false)
}
```

```
    pub compare_step: Option<String>,
    /// Shape loaded from the --compare STEP file, for bounded distance checks.
    pub compare_shape: Option<opencascade_sys::OwnedPtr<opencascade_sys::topo_ds::Shape>>,
    /// Number of faces in the compare shape (for status output).
    pub compare_face_count: usize,
    /// Fitting tolerance for vertex-to-surface distance, in mm.
    pub vertex_tolerance_mm: f64,
    /// Tolerance for surface-to-triangle-face offset, in mm.
    pub surface_tolerance_mm: f64,
    /// Max dihedral angle (radians) between adjacent triangles on the same surface.
    pub angular_tolerance_rad: f64,
    /// Verbosity level (0 = quiet-ish, 1 = verbose, 2 = very verbose, 3 = BFS trace).
    pub verbosity: u8,
    /// Convenience: verbosity >= 1.
    pub verbose: bool,
    /// Suppress non-error output.
    pub quiet: bool,
    /// Enable debug output and intermediate files.
    pub debug: bool,
    /// Stop after this stage.
    pub stage: Stage,
    /// Stages to visualize interactively.
    pub viz_stages: Vec<Stage>,
}

impl std::fmt::Debug for Config {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("Config")
            .field("input_stl", &self.input_stl)
            .field("output_step", &self.output_step)
            .field("stl_units", &self.stl_units)
            .field("step_units", &self.step_units)
            .field("compare_step", &self.compare_step)
            .field("compare_shape", &if self.compare_shape.is_some() { format!("[{} faces]", self.compare_face_count) } else { "None".to_string() })
            .field("vertex_tolerance_mm", &self.vertex_tolerance_mm)
            .field("surface_tolerance_mm", &self.surface_tolerance_mm)
            .field("angular_tolerance_rad", &self.angular_tolerance_rad)
            .field("verbosity", &self.verbosity)
            .field("verbose", &self.verbose)
            .field("quiet", &self.quiet)
            .field("debug", &self.debug)
            .field("stage", &self.stage)
            .field("viz_stages", &self.viz_stages)
            .finish()
    }
}

impl Config {
    fn from_cli(args: CliArgs) -> Self {
        let scale = args.stl_units.to_mm();
        Self {
            input_stl: args.input_stl,
            output_step: args.output_step,
            stl_units: args.stl_units,
            step_units: args.step_units,
            compare_step: args.compare_step,
            compare_shape: None,
            compare_face_count: 0,
            vertex_tolerance_mm: args.vertex_tolerance * scale,
            surface_tolerance_mm: args.surface_tolerance * scale,
            angular_tolerance_rad: args.angular_tolerance.to_radians(),
            verbosity: args.verbose_level.unwrap_or(0).max(args.verbose_short),
            verbose: args.verbose_level.unwrap_or(0).max(args.verbose_short) >= 1,
            quiet: args.quiet,
            debug: args.debug,
            stage: args.stage.unwrap_or_default(),
            viz_stages: args.viz_stages.unwrap_or_default(),
        }
    }

    /// Check if a specific stage should be visualized.
    pub fn viz_active(&self, major: u8, minor: u8) -> bool {
        self.viz_stages.iter().any(|s| {
            let (sm, ss) = s.normalized();
            sm == major && ss == minor
        })
    }

    /// If --compare was specified, load the STEP file shape for bounded distance comparison.
    pub fn load_compare_step(&mut self) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
```
</details>

> 💭 OK. Now I see the structure. `config.quiet` is a separate flag, and `config.verbose` is `verbosity >= 1`. The user's spec says:
> - v=1: stage summaries → currently behind `!config.quiet`
> - v=2: per-hypothesis details → currently behind `config.verbose` (which is v>=1)
> 
> So I need to change:
> 1. `!config.quiet` → `config.verbosity >= 1` for stage summaries
> 2. `config.verbose` → `config.verbosity >= 2` for per-hypothesis details
> 3. Per-face sphere details at `config.verbosity >= 2` → `config.verbosity >= 3`
> 
> 
> 4. Update the verbosity level descriptions in config.rs to match what the user actually specified: v=1 for stage summaries, v=2 for hypothesis details with indentation, and v=3 for seed face and final face counts. I'm realizing there's a tension here—the user defined verbosity levels starting at 1 for summaries, but the current code shows summaries at verbosity=0 when quiet mode isn't enabled. If I strictly follow the new scheme, summaries would only appear with `-v`, which changes the default behavior and might break existing workflows. I need to figure out whether the user intended to preserve the current default (summaries always shown unless `-q` is used) or truly require `-v` for any output.

Now let me update mod.rs. I'll change  →  for stage summaries, and  for hypothesis details:

```
    fn viz_overlays(
        fit: &Self::Fit,
        mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
        face_list: &[usize],
    ) -> (Vec<viz::CylinderOverlay>, Vec<viz::ConeOverlay>, Vec<viz::SphereOverlay>, Vec<viz::TorusOverlay>) {
        if fit.half_angle.abs() < 1e-6 {
            // Cylinder
            let cyl = centered_cylinder_overlay(
                fit.apex, fit.axis_direction, fit.radius,
                face_list, mesh,
                [0.2, 0.4, 1.0, 0.3],
            );
            (vec![cyl], Vec::new(), Vec::new(), Vec::new())
        } else {
            // True cone
            let cone = centered_cone_overlay(
```

```
pub(crate) fn centered_cone_overlay(
    apex: [f64; 3], direction: [f64; 3], half_angle: f64,
    face_list: &[usize], mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    color: [f32; 4],
) -> viz::ConeOverlay {
    // Compute axial extent of face centroids relative to apex
    let mut t_min = f64::INFINITY;
    let mut t_max = f64::NEG_INFINITY;
    for &fi in face_list {
        let c = face_centroid(&mesh.faces[fi], &mesh.vertices);
        let d = [c[0] - apex[0], c[1] - apex[1], c[2] - apex[2]];
        let t = dot3(&d, &direction);
        t_min = t_min.min(t);
        t_max = t_max.max(t);
    }
    // Ensure the cone is at least 2x the maximum model dimension in length
    let bb_diag = bounding_box_diagonal(&mesh.vertices);
    let min_half_length = bb_diag; // 2x max dimension means full length = 2 * bb_diag, so half_length = bb_diag
    let center_t = (t_min + t_max) * 0.5;
    let half_span = ((t_max - t_min) * 0.5).max(min_half_length);
    // Pad by 20% for visual clarity
    let pad = half_span * 0.2;
    viz::ConeOverlay {
        apex,
        direction,
        half_angle,
        t_min: center_t - half_span - pad,
        t_max: center_t + half_span + pad,
        color,
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Stage 2.6: Select surfaces for reconstruction
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Greedy area-based surface selection.
///
/// Selects hypotheses greedily by total mesh face area (largest first).
/// This naturally resolves conflicts: real surface hypotheses cover many faces
/// with large total area, while bogus hypotheses cover minimal area and lose.
///
/// After selection, updates hypothesis face/vertex lists to reflect only the
/// faces actually assigned to each selected surface.
fn select_surfaces(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    planar_hypotheses: &mut [PlanarHypothesis],
    conical_hypotheses: &mut [ConicalHypothesis],
    spherical_hypotheses: &mut [SphericalHypothesis],
    toroidal_hypotheses: &mut [ToroidalHypothesis],
    viz: Option<&VizSender>,
) -> Vec<SelectedSurface> {
    let num_faces = mesh.faces.len();

    // Step 1: Compute geometric area of each mesh face.
    let face_areas: Vec<f64> = (0..num_faces)
        .map(|fi| face_area(&mesh.faces[fi], &mesh.vertices))
        .collect();

    // Step 2: Build candidate list from multi-face hypotheses.
    struct Candidate {
        surface_type: u8, // 0=planar, 1=conical(cylinder), 2=spherical, 3=conical(cone), 4=toroidal
        hypothesis_index: usize,
        faces: Vec<usize>,
    }
```

<details>
<summary>Reading lines 525-575 of <a href="../src/stage2/mod.rs#L525-L576">mod.rs</a></summary>

```
pub(crate) fn centered_cylinder_overlay(
    origin: [f64; 3], direction: [f64; 3], radius: f64,
    face_list: &[usize], mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    color: [f32; 4],
) -> viz::CylinderOverlay {
    // Compute full mesh bounding box extent for cylinder length
    let mut bb_min = [f64::INFINITY; 3];
    let mut bb_max = [f64::NEG_INFINITY; 3];
    for v in &mesh.vertices {
        let coords = [v.x, v.y, v.z];
        for i in 0..3 {
            bb_min[i] = bb_min[i].min(coords[i]);
            bb_max[i] = bb_max[i].max(coords[i]);
        }
    }
    let extent = ((bb_max[0] - bb_min[0]).powi(2)
        + (bb_max[1] - bb_min[1]).powi(2)
        + (bb_max[2] - bb_min[2]).powi(2))
    .sqrt();

    // Center on face list's axial extent
    let mut t_min = f64::INFINITY;
    let mut t_max = f64::NEG_INFINITY;
    for &fi in face_list {
        let c = face_centroid(&mesh.faces[fi], &mesh.vertices);
        let d = [c[0] - origin[0], c[1] - origin[1], c[2] - origin[2]];
        let t = dot3(&d, &direction);
        t_min = t_min.min(t);
        t_max = t_max.max(t);
    }
    let t_mid = (t_min + t_max) * 0.5;
    let half_len = extent;
    let centered_origin = [
        origin[0] + direction[0] * t_mid,
        origin[1] + direction[1] * t_mid,
        origin[2] + direction[2] * t_mid,
    ];
    viz::CylinderOverlay {
        origin: centered_origin,
        direction,
        radius,
        half_length: half_len,
        color,
    }
}

/// Ensures the cone is at least 2x the maximum model dimension in length.
```

```
// Config (the resolved, normalized configuration used by the pipeline)
```

```
/// Resolved configuration used by the pipeline. Tolerances are stored in mm.
pub struct Config {
    /// Input STL file path.
    pub input_stl: String,
    /// Output STEP file path.
    pub output_step: Option<String>,
    /// Units of the input STL file.
    pub stl_units: Units,
    /// Units for the output STEP file.
    pub step_units: Units,
    /// STEP file path to compare against at each stage.
    pub compare_step: Option<String>,
    /// Shape loaded from the --compare STEP file, for bounded distance checks.
    pub compare_shape: Option<opencascade_sys::OwnedPtr<opencascade_sys::topo_ds::Shape>>,
    /// Number of faces in the compare shape (for status output).
    pub compare_face_count: usize,
    /// Fitting tolerance for vertex-to-surface distance, in mm.
    pub vertex_tolerance_mm: f64,
    /// Tolerance for surface-to-triangle-face offset, in mm.
    pub surface_tolerance_mm: f64,
    /// Max dihedral angle (radians) between adjacent triangles on the same surface.
    pub angular_tolerance_rad: f64,
    /// Verbosity level (0 = quiet, 1 = summaries, 2 = hypotheses, 3 = commit details, 4 = seed details, 5 = BFS trace).
    pub verbosity: u8,
    /// Convenience: verbosity >= 1.
    pub verbose: bool,
    /// Suppress non-error output.
    pub quiet: bool,
    /// Enable debug output and intermediate files.
    pub debug: bool,
    /// Stop after this stage.
    pub stage: Stage,
    /// Stages to visualize interactively.
    pub viz_stages: Vec<Stage>,
}

impl std::fmt::Debug for Config {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("Config")
            .field("input_stl", &self.input_stl)
            .field("output_step", &self.output_step)
            .field("stl_units", &self.stl_units)
            .field("step_units", &self.step_units)
            .field("compare_step", &self.compare_step)
            .field("compare_shape", &if self.compare_shape.is_some() { format!("[{} faces]", self.compare_face_count) } else { "None".to_string() })
            .field("vertex_tolerance_mm", &self.vertex_tolerance_mm)
            .field("surface_tolerance_mm", &self.surface_tolerance_mm)
            .field("angular_tolerance_rad", &self.angular_tolerance_rad)
            .field("verbosity", &self.verbosity)
            .field("verbose", &self.verbose)
            .field("quiet", &self.quiet)
            .field("debug", &self.debug)
            .field("stage", &self.stage)
            .field("viz_stages", &self.viz_stages)
            .finish()
    }
}
```

````
    --compare=<step>         STEP file to compare to at each step
    --vertex-tolerance=<value>  Fitting tolerance in STL units (default: 5e-5)
    --surface-tolerance=<value> Surface-to-face offset tolerance in STL units (default: 0.4)
    --angular-tolerance=<deg>   Max dihedral angle between adjacent triangles on the same surface (default: 17.5)
    -v, --verbose[=LEVEL]    Verbosity level: 1 (or bare -v) = summaries, -vv or --verbose=2 = per-face details, -vvv or --verbose=3 = full BFS trace
    -q, --quiet              Suppress non-error output
    --debug                  Enable debug output and intermediate files
    --stage=<stage>          Stop after stage, e.g. 2.2 (default: 4.1). --stage=2 stops after all of stage2
    --viz-stages=<stages>    Open interactive 3D visualization at specified stages (e.g. 2.1,2.2,2.3)
```

# Full conversion

# Just load and validate mesh (stage 1.2)
./target/debug/brepper input.stl --stage 1.2 -v

# Compare mesh against reference STEP at each stage
````

Now let me also update the viz-stages section of README:

<details>
<summary>Reading lines 88-110 of <a href="../README.md#L88-L111">README.md</a></summary>

````
**Important:** `box(w,d,h)` places a corner at the origin, not centered. Use `center_xyz()` to center. Each part file includes analytical surface area and volume in its header comments.

### Interactive Debugging Visualization

Use `--viz-stages` to open a 3D window and step through BFS hypothesis deduction interactively:

```bash
# Visualize planar and cylindrical hypothesis stages
brepper input.stl --viz-stages=2.1,2.2

# Visualize all stage 2 hypothesis types with STEP comparison overlay
brepper input.stl --compare reference.step --viz-stages=2.1,2.2,2.3
```

At each BFS step, the window shows the STL mesh with highlighted faces: seed faces in green, previously accepted faces in blue, the just-accepted face in dark blue, and BFS queue frontier edges in red. Cylindrical and spherical hypotheses also display translucent geometry overlays. When `--compare` is provided, the STEP surface is shown at 35% opacity for reference.

| Action | Control |
|--------|:--------|
| Next BFS step | `Space` |
| Skip to next seed/hypothesis | `Shift+Space` |
| Quit visualization | `Q` |

Supported stages: `2.1` (planar), `2.2` (cylindrical), `2.3` (spherical), `2.4` (conical), `2.6` (surface selection), `3.3` (edge curves), `3.4` (faces), `3.5` (shells), `3.6` (solids).
````

<details>
<summary>Reading lines 265-275 of <a href="../DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md#L265-L276">DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md</a></summary>

```

**Seed type**: Three faces `(fi, f1, f2)` — the starting face `fi`, a pairwise-qualified neighbor `f1` of `fi`, and another neighbor `f2` of `fi` where `f2` is NOT an edge-wise neighbor of `f1` (so `fi` is between `f1` and `f2` rather than at one end of a chain). Both `f1` and `f2` must be pairwise-qualified with `fi`. This places the seed faces at different circumferential positions around `fi`, providing angular diversity for the fit. Multiple seeds may be generated per starting face (one per valid `(f1, f2)` pair); the best trial by area is kept.

*Pairwise qualification*: Two faces are pairwise qualified if they are neighbors, both unassigned for this surface type (`conical_hypothesis < 0`), have sufficiently different normals (cross product magnitude exceeds `MIN_CROSS_THRESHOLD`, e.g. 0.01), and the dihedral angle does not exceed `--angular-tolerance`. The cross product minimum is a seeding criterion only — during BFS expansion, adjacent faces on a finely tessellated cylinder legitimately have near-parallel normals.

**Surface fit**: Attempts both a cone fit (5-parameter) and a cylinder fit (4-parameter) independently, then selects whichever achieves the lower maximum vertex-to-surface distance. If the cone's fitted half-angle is outside the valid range (2°–85°) or its apex is too far from the mesh, the cone fit is rejected. The fits use: (1) Axis direction from smallest eigenvector of the area-weighted normal covariance matrix $M = \sum w_i n_i n_i^T$. (2) For cylinders: project vertices perpendicular to the axis, fit a 2D circle for axis position and radius. For cones: estimate half-angle and apex from vertex positions along the axis. (3) LM refinement minimizing vertex-to-surface distances, with vertices **recentered** around their centroid for numerical stability (parameters shifted back after fitting), and `patience=500` to ensure convergence well below vertex tolerance. With `--verbose 3` or higher, each seed fit logs both the cone and cylinder fit attempts along with their max vertex/surface errors and rejection reasons. See [SURFACE_FITTING.md](SURFACE_FITTING.md) for detailed fitting math.

**Distance metric**: For cylinders (axis origin `o`, axis direction `a`, radius `r`): distance from point `p` is $| \|p - o - ((p-o)\cdot a)a\| - r |$. For cones (apex, axis, half_angle): distance is the point-to-cone-surface distance along the radial direction.

**BFS-specific check — convexity**: Compute the vector from the axis to the face's centroid. Check that the face's normal agrees with the hypothesis convexity (dot product with radial vector has the expected sign). Reject if inconsistent — this prevents merging the inner and outer surfaces of a thin-walled cylinder.

```

<summary>Reading lines 616-685 of <a href="../DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md#L616-L686">DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md</a></summary>

````
## Visualization (`--viz-stages`)

The `--viz-stages` flag enables interactive 3D visualization for debugging each pipeline stage. The user specifies a stage pattern (e.g. `2.2` for conical BFS, or `2.*` for all stage 2 sub-stages).

### Architecture

The visualization uses a two-thread architecture with synchronous channel communication:

1. **Main thread**: Owns the window, drives the three-d render loop, and displays overlays.
2. **Pipeline thread**: Runs the brepper pipeline (stages 1–4) in the background.

### Communication pipeline

```
Pipeline thread                          Main/render thread
     │                                         │
     │  VizOverlay (via SyncSender)             │
     ├────────────────────────────────────────►  │
     │                                         │ display overlay, wait for keypress
     │  VizAction (via SyncSender)              │
     │  ◄────────────────────────────────────────┤
     │                                         │
     ▼                                         ▼
```

- `VizSender::show_and_wait(overlay)` sends a `VizOverlay` to the render thread and blocks.
- The render thread displays face highlights, translucent geometry overlays (cylinders, cones, spheres, tori), status text, and optionally moves the camera.
- The user presses **Space** (NextStep), **Shift+Space** (NextSeed/skip to next seed), or **Q** (Quit).
- The render thread sends back a `VizAction` and the pipeline thread resumes.

### Key types (in `src/viz.rs`)

- `VizSender` / `VizReceiver`: Channel endpoints. Created by `viz_channel()`.
- `VizOverlay`: A display frame containing face highlights, cylinder/cone/sphere/torus overlays, line overlays, shape meshes, status text, and optional camera focus.
- `VizAction`: The user's response — `NextStep`, `NextSeed`, `SkipToSeed(face_index)`, or `Quit`.
- `FaceHighlight`, `CylinderOverlay`, `ConeOverlay`, `SphereOverlay`, `TorusOverlay`, `LineOverlay`, `ShapeMeshOverlay`: Overlay primitives.

### Interactive features

The render window (powered by three-d with egui overlay) provides:
- **Camera controls**: Left-drag orbit, right-drag pan, scroll zoom (toward cursor).
- **Face numbers** (`N` key): Thin black labels at face centroids, culled by face-normal direction (back-facing labels are hidden). The hovered face number is displayed larger with a white halo.
- **Hover info**: Ray-cast face picking under the cursor displays face index, vertex count, area, and normal in the bottom-left corner. Thick bright-yellow edges are drawn around the hovered face.
- **Click-to-skip**: Left-clicking a face sends `SkipToSeed(face_index)`, causing the BFS loop to skip ahead to the seed containing that face. Click is only processed while the window is paused waiting for user input (`waiting_for_input = true`) to avoid a deadlock where the BFS thread would block trying to send the next overlay while the render loop also blocks waiting for input.
- **Help overlay** (`?` key): Shows all keyboard and mouse shortcuts.

### Integration with generic BFS

The `SurfaceType` trait provides:
- `viz_overlays(fit, mesh, face_list)`: Returns geometry overlays (e.g., a translucent cylinder for conical fits, a sphere for spherical fits).
- `viz_fit_description(fit)`: Returns a short text description of the fit parameters for the status bar.

The generic `generate_hypotheses()` function in `src/stage2/bfs.rs` sends viz overlays at two points:
1. **Seed display**: After a seed is validated, shows the seed faces with the initial fit overlay.
2. **BFS accept**: After each face accepted during BFS expansion, shows the growing region with the updated fit.

When `viz` is `None` (the common case), all visualization is skipped with zero overhead.

---
````

```
      "content": "**Surface fit**: Attempts both a cone fit (5-parameter) and a cylinder fit (4-parameter) independently, then selects whichever achieves the lower maximum vertex-to-surface distance. If the cone's fitted half-angle is outside the valid range (2°–85°) or its apex is too far from the mesh, the cone fit is rejected. The fits use: (1) Axis direction from smallest eigenvector of the area-weighted normal covariance matrix $M = \\sum w_i n_i n_i^T$. (2) For cylinders: project vertices perpendicular to the axis, fit a 2D circle for axis position and radius. For cones: estimate half-angle and apex from vertex positions along the axis. (3) LM refinement minimizing vertex-to-surface distances, with vertices **recentered** around their centroid for numerical stability (parameters shifted back after fitting), and `patience=500` to ensure convergence well below vertex tolerance. With `--verbose 4` or higher, each seed fit logs both the cone and cylinder fit attempts along with their max vertex/surface errors and rejection reasons. ## Visualization (`--viz-stages`)
```

```
The `--viz-stages` flag enables interactive 3D visualization for debugging each pipeline stage. The user specifies a stage pattern (e.g. `2.2` for conical BFS, or `2.*` for all stage 2 sub-stages).

### Architecture

The visualization uses a two-thread architecture with synchronous channel communication:

1. **Main thread**: Owns the window, drives the three-d render loop, and displays overlays.
2. **Pipeline thread**: Runs the brepper pipeline (stages 1–4) in the background.
```
```

## Toroidal hypothesis visualization
Added torus overlay rendering to the interactive viz. Toroidal hypotheses now display as translucent torus surfaces instead of cylinders at the major radius. Added `TorusOverlay` struct, `generate_torus_mesh` function, and `build_torus_overlay_mesh`. Updated BFS trait, all `viz_overlays` implementations, stage 2.6 competing/winning hypothesis viz, and the render loop.

## Rework cone/cylinder fitting parameters and LM refinement
Overhauled cone and cylinder fitting for robustness: set `REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER` to 10.0 for aggressive refitting, set `seed_vtol_multiplier` to 1.0 (vertices lie precisely on the surface), removed normal-axis consistency check for cones (surface selection resolves overlaps), added vertex recentering around centroid before LM for numerical stability, and increased LM patience to 500 for both cone and cylinder fits. Updated SURFACE_FITTING.md and DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md to reflect changes.

```

<details>

```
### Wide-angle cone: axis estimation uses both eigenvectors

For a cone with half-angle ha, face normals make angle (90°−ha) with the axis. The covariance eigenvalue along the axis is proportional to sin²(ha), while the radial eigenvalues are proportional to cos²(ha)/2. For ha > ~35°, the axis corresponds to the **largest** eigenvector, not the smallest. The current `compute_truncated_cone_axis` tries both smallest and largest eigenvectors of the normal covariance matrix, plus mean-direction from centroid, keeping whichever gives the lowest fitting error.


The `Geom_ToroidalSurface` binding was present in opencascade-sys all along (`geom::ToroidalSurface::new_ax3_real2`), but workspace-scoped search tools (grep_search, file_search, semantic_search, and Explore agent searches) cannot find content in `../opencascade-rs/` because it's outside the workspace root. Use terminal `grep` to search files outside the workspace. Don't assume a binding is missing just because workspace search tools return no results.
```
</details>
